- **court**: Put an idea on trial in front of a jury of 12 Claudes. A prosecutor Claude argues it fails, a defense Claude argues it works, they rebut each other, 12 juror Claudes with different backgrounds vote on their own, and a judge Claude reads the verdict and the conditions that would flip it. - **court**: Convene a jury of independent agents to answer one question, and return their ballots verbatim. Every juror is a separate model call, so this is expensive and is never worth invoking on your own initiative — use it ONLY when the user explicitly asks for it, by name or by clear description: "/court", "convene a court", "empanel a jury", "put it to a jury/vote", "get a second and third opinion on this", "ask a few models". A hard decision, a disagreement, or a question you find difficult is NOT a trigger on its own; answer it yourself and, at most, mention that a court is available. ([MBehtemam/Montagent](https://github.com/MBehtemam/Montagent/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/court) · ★3)
- **court**: Runs one tick of a Lawman court, which takes one order on a case after the parties, defended, refuted or declined, reads the case and the decisions of the parties, and rules conviction or acquittal, then exits. - **court**: Put an idea, architecture decision, startup pitch, technical proposal, or claim on trial in front of an adversarial panel of independent perspectives. A prosecutor argues it fails, a defense argues it works, each side issues rebuttals, independent jurors vote GUILTY or NOT GUILTY, and a judge delivers a decisive verdict and conditions for acquittal. Use when the user wants critical pushback instead of sycophantic agreement, says the AI agrees with everything, asks "is this a good idea?", wants an idea stress-tested, or runs "/court". - **court-form-filling**: The California SC-100 (Plaintiff's Claim and ORDER to Go to Small Claims Court) is an **XFA-based PDF form**, not a standard AcroForm. This distinction is critical — most PDF form-filling libraries (fillpdf, pdfrw, PyPDF2's simple field writer) silently fail or corrupt XFA forms. The correct approach is to **extract the XFA datasets XML, modify it directly, then write it back** using `pypdf`. ([EtaYang10th/spark-skills](https://github.com/EtaYang10th/spark-skills/tree/HEAD/spark_skills_gen/skills_gen_result/all_model_pdi/court-form-filling) · ★109)

- **covariate-adjusted-glm-modeling**: Use when performing transcriptomics differential expression testing on RNA-seq count matrices where sample metadata contains confounders (e.g. CONDITION, SEX, AGE columns) that must be included in the design formula before filtering significant genes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/covariate-adjusted-glm-modeling))
- **covariate-adjusted-linear-modeling-ancova**: Use when a metabolomics or proteomics feature table has study covariates (metadata beyond the primary group label) and you need to test whether covariate adjustment changes which features are statistically significant versus plain ANOVA/t-tests. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/covariate-adjusted-linear-modeling-ancova))
- **covariate-adjusted-linear-regression**: Use when comparing treatment groups on a continuous microbiome outcome (alpha-diversity index or CLR-transformed abundance) while adjusting for covariates such as Sex, as in MiSurv Module 2. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/covariate-adjusted-linear-regression))
- **covariate-adjusted-linear-regression-fdr**: Use when you have per-cell module-specific expression scores from a GRN (e.g. SCING Leiden modules scored with AUCell) plus per-cell clinical metadata (trait labels and confounders such as sex), and need FDR-controlled module-trait association statistics. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/covariate-adjusted-linear-regression-fdr))
- **covariate-adjusted-lipidomics-statistics**: Use when a lipidomics dataset (e.g. Metabolomics Workbench study ST001111) has loaded clinical variables and the differential-abundance contrast may be confounded by a covariate such as Race, requiring a factorial model like ~ Race + SampleType. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/covariate-adjusted-lipidomics-statistics))
- **covariate-adjusted-longitudinal-modeling**: Use when the input is a longitudinal master table (features x samples over repeated time points, e.g. genus-level fasting stool data with a Day or Time_point variable) and time effects must be separated from covariate effects in count, proportion, binary, ordinal or continuous data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/covariate-adjusted-longitudinal-modeling))
- **covariate-adjusted-mirkat-s-analysis**: Use when you have an OTU/feature table with matched survival times, censoring/event status, and covariates, and need a kernel-based beta-diversity-vs-survival association test (MiRKAT-S), optionally within a treatment-group subgroup. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/covariate-adjusted-mirkat-s-analysis))
- **covariate-adjusted-prediction-modeling**: Use when the goal is a supervised classification model (e.g., breast cancer vs. control) from metabolomics and/or gene-expression data where covariate adjustment and feature filtering must be demonstrated against reference metrics like Q2Y = 0.701. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/covariate-adjusted-prediction-modeling))
- **covariate-adjusted-regression-modeling**: Use when testing host genetic variants (SNVs/SNPs) for association with microbial taxon abundances across a sample cohort and covariates (e.g., covariates.txt/CovFile) must be adjusted for in the regression model. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/covariate-adjusted-regression-modeling))
- **covariate-adjusted-spatial-modeling**: Use when analyzing spatially-resolved transcriptomics data (e.g., SpatialExperiment objects) where known spatial domain labels exist and you need to detect SVGs adjusted for domain structure, or benchmark SVG detection against SPARK-X, HVGs, and Moran's I. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/covariate-adjusted-spatial-modeling))
- **covariate-adjustment-confounder-control**: Use when scanning ~249 NMR metabolic biomarkers (plus derived ratios) for associations with incident CAD or stroke in UK Biobank, and results must be adjusted for age and sex with prevalent cases and lipid-lowering medication users excluded. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/covariate-adjustment-confounder-control))
- **covariate-adjustment-via-linear-regression-residuals**: Use when raw metabolomics peak intensities (e.g., Metabolomics Workbench ST001386/ST001636 TEDDY data) contain demographic covariates such as age and sex that confound a case/control contrast, before creating the DNEA object. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/covariate-adjustment-via-linear-regression-residuals))
- **covariate-association-agreement-testing**: Use when validating quality of feature matches between two untargeted LC-MS metabolomics datasets by checking whether matched feature pairs carry consistent covariate associations (age, BMI, gender) in both datasets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/covariate-association-agreement-testing))
- **covariate-confounding-benchmark-design**: Use when benchmarking longitudinal microbiome differential-abundance tools under covariate-time confounding, or when deciding whether a tool's FDR/TPR is robust to increasing numbers of dummy covariates. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/covariate-confounding-benchmark-design))
- **covariate-effect-success-rate-evaluation**: Use when comparing longitudinal differential-abundance tools (e.g. LongDat vs MaAsLin2) on simulated multi-covariate data and you need to quantify whether covariate control keeps FDR stable and covariate-effect success rate near 0.95. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/covariate-effect-success-rate-evaluation))
- **covariate-encoded-trajectory-modelling**: Use when a cross-sectional single-cell expression cohort carries a sample-level covariate (stimulant, mutation, drug) that may modulate progression, and pseudotime must be inferred without explicit time-series labels. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/covariate-encoded-trajectory-modelling))
- **covariate-gene-interaction-significance-testing**: Use when a cross-sectional expression cohort (e.g. TCGA-BRCA log2(TPM+1) matrix of 4579 HVGs x 1135 samples) must be ordered along a pseudotemporal trajectory while testing which genes' loadings interact with a sample covariate such as ER status. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/covariate-gene-interaction-significance-testing))
- **covariate-pseudotime-interaction-ranking**: Use when a PhenoPath fit is available and the goal is to rank genes by covariate-pseudotime interaction effect size and identify significant interactions (e.g. Shalek LPS/PAM genes, TCGA MSI analysis). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/covariate-pseudotime-interaction-ranking))
- **covariate-reducibility-classification**: Use when analyzing longitudinal high-dimensional data (e.g. gut microbial abundance over Day or Time_point) where a significant time effect on a feature must be checked against potentially confounding covariates before being declared a treatment effect. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/covariate-reducibility-classification))
- **covariate-regression-from-gene-expression**: Use when comparing the wall-clock scalability of nuisance-covariate removal methods (SiFT, scVI, scanpy regress_out) on large scRNA-seq data, e.g. continuous covariates regressed from the expression matrix across subsamples up to ~500,000 cells. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/covariate-regression-from-gene-expression))
- **covariate-regression-on-sample-parameters**: Use when you have a fitted GEDI model for a multi-sample single-cell dataset (e.g., PBMCsca) and need to visualize sample-level manifold parameters or verify that GEDI disentangled donor from technology effects, as in Fig. 2a/2d UMAPs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/covariate-regression-on-sample-parameters))
- **covariate-regression-residualization-limma**: Use when a per-cell technical covariate (e.g. dsb's PC1 of isotype controls and fitted Gaussian-mixture background means µ1) must be regressed out of normalized protein/ADT values to return denoised residuals. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/covariate-regression-residualization-limma))
- **covariate-taxon-association-extraction-from-mppi**: Use when you have MCMC posterior samples from a Dirichlet-multinomial (DM) regression fit in MicroBVS and need to declare which covariate-taxon associations are selected, e.g. against a simulated ground truth. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/covariate-taxon-association-extraction-from-mppi))
